#### 1st Level: Weapon Binding
You learn to imprint your Arts onto a Weapon to better manipulate it. 
:
**Binding Ritual.** Over the course of ten minutes, which can be done during a Rest, you can bind to a Melee Weapon. The weapon must be within your reach throughout the ritual, at the conclusion of which you touch the weapon and forge the bond, granting it the Bound Property while it is bonded. The bond fails if another Creature is bonded to it, or if the weapon is a magic item to which someone else is attuned. 
:
The maximum number of Bound Weapons you can have is 1; this is increased to 2 at Level 5, and 3 at Level 14.
:
**Using a Bound Weapon.** A Weapon Bound to you floats in an empty space as close to you as possible, moving when you do, until commanded otherwise. When taking the Attack Action, instead of making your normal Attacks, you can command each of your Bound Weapons to move up to 30 feet in any direction to a location you can see; Weapons can move through Creature's spaces during this movement, but cannot finish this movement in a Creature's space. The Bound Weapon then floats at this location.
:
After this movement, you can make a Melee Attack with each of your Bound Weapons that has a Creature within 5 feet of it (10 feet if the Bound Weapon has the Reach Property). These Weapon Attacks use your Spellcasting Ability Modifier (instead of Strength or Dexterity) for the Attack and Damage rolls, and you are considered to be Proficient with those Weapons for these Attacks. 
:
Additionally, if you have cast a levelled Art during your Turn, until the end of that Turn you can move one of your Bound  Weapons (but not Attack with it) as a Bonus Action.
:
**Opportunity Attacks with Bound Weapons.** If a Creature would voluntarily leave the reach of one of your Bound Weapons, you may make an Attack of Opportunity against them with one of those Bound Weapons; this does not consume your Reaction, but once a Bound Weapon has made an Attack of Opportunity, it cannot do so again until the start of your next Turn.
:
**Recalling Bound Weapons.** You can recall all of your Bound Weapons within 100 feet to your side as an Action; they resume floating in an empty space as close to you as possible, and move when you do. If a Bound Weapon is ever more than 100 feet away from you, your Arts lose their hold, and it ceases to be Bound to you.

\column

#### 4th Level: Aftershock
The arrival of your Bound Weapon is almost as dangerous as their strikes. 
:
After you have moved your Bound Weapons, all hostile Creatures within 10 feet of the Bound Weapon's new location take Thunder Damage equal to your Spellcasting Ability Modifier. A Creature can only take this Damage once per Turn.

<img 
 src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/Greyy_the_Lightningbearer.png?cfa39a' 
  style='position:absolute;top:250px;right:10px;width:400px' />

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide



#### 10th Level: Kinetic Strike
You can focus all of your might on a singular enemy in a concerted effort to destroy them utterly.
:
An an Action, choose a Creature you can see within 100 feet; that Creature must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Spell Save DC. On a failed Save, they take 4d8 Arts Bludgeoning Damage for each Bound Weapon you have. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage. All of your Bound Weapons are then placed in empty spaces as close to that Creature as possible.
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have completed a Long Rest.

#### 15th Level: Pass-through Strike
Whenever you move your Bound Weapons through the space of a Hostile Creature, that Creature takes Piercing Damage equal to your Spellcasting Ability Modifier. A Creature can only take this Damage once per Turn.

#### 18th Level: Whirling Death
Your control over your Bound Weapons is worthy of study; your might capable of changing the course of history.
:
As an Action during your Turn, you can whirl your Bound Weapons into a slicing maelstrom around you; this maelstrom is a 50-foot-radius Sphere centred on you for the duration. When this Sphere appears, you may choose any number of Creatures to be unaffected by it. Until the end of your next Turn, when this Sphere enters a affected Creature's space, or they end their Turn there, they must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Spell Save DC. On a failed Save, they take 8d8 Slashing Damage; on a successful Save, they take half as much. Once a Creature has made this Saving Throw, they do not need to make it again until the beginning of your next Turn.
:
You cannot move your Bound Weapons or Attack with them while Whirling Death is ongoing. As an Action during your Turn, you may extend the duration of Whirling Death until the end of your next Turn; Whirling Death has a maximum duration of 3 Turns. 
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have finished a Long Rest.
}}

{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:2%,--rotation:0
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/Wi%C5%A1%27adel_Elite_2.png?b739cf' 
  style='position:absolute;top:190px;right:-310px;width:1300px;clip-path:rect(29% auto auto auto);' />
}}



\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

### Heavyshot
In many ways, there are similarities between Deadeye Marksmen and Heavyshot Marksmen. Both believe in definite action and decisive strikes, but differ in how it should be delivered. For a Heavyshot Marksman, it is not a question of applying their power in precision, but in applying the most strength in the shortest time. They strike fast and without mercy, blowing away chaff and hard targets alike, wielding custom weapons better suited to their "shock and awe" tactics.

\column

#### 1st Level: Weapon Tuning
You learn how to get the absolute most out of your weapon- even if it isn't what could be called "safe".
:
As a Long Rest Activity, you may modify a Heavy Ranged Weapon, turning it into a Custom Job. A Custom Job gains the following effects:

**Exploding Damage.** Whenever Damage is rolled for an Attack with that Weapon, if one of the dice results is the maximum result possible on that dice, the attacker may roll an additional die of the same size, and add the result to the Damage. The maximum number of dice that can be added is equal to the attacker's Proficiency Bonus.

**Heaviest Shot.** If that Weapon has the Loading Property, it deals an additional die of Damage.

**Shortened Range.** The Normal and Long Range values of that Weapon are halved.
:
As a Result of your Modifications, only you can handle your Custom Job- all other Creatures are not considered Proficient with a Custom Job, even if they would normally have Proficiency with the Weapon. 


#### 1st Level: Devastator
Your Attacks strike with such force that they can punch through multiple foes. 
:
When you make a Ranged Attack against a Creature within the Normal Range of that Weapon, that Attack has +1 Critical Range. Additionally, if you reduce that Creature to 0 Hit Points with an Attack using your Custom Job, and there is another Creature that is:
- Behind the original Creature from your perspective.
- Within 10 feet of the original Creature.
- Has an AC equal to or less than the Attack Roll made for that Attack.

{{align-text:left
That Creature then takes any excess Damage from the initial Attack. If the Creature reduced to 0 was your Target, then roll an additional die of Damage for that Attack, and add it to the excess Damage taken by the second Creature.
}}



{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:2%,--rotation:90
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/d/d2/OD-Schwarz_Takes_Aim_1.png?7bad37' 
  style='position:absolute;top:250px;right:-200px;width:1500px;;border-radius: 100%;' />
}}

{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:22%,--rotation:0
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/d/d2/OD-Schwarz_Takes_Aim_1.png?7bad37' 
  style='position:absolute;top:250px;right:-200px;width:1500px;;border-radius: 100%;' />
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

#### 4th Level: Savage Shots
You may use this Feature as a Bonus Action; until the end of the Turn, when you deal Damage with your Custom Job against a Target within its Normal Range, you may roll any number of those dice again, but must use the new result.
:
You may use this Feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you complete a Long Rest.

#### 10th Level: Improved Tuning
You have improved your "craftsmanship", and gain the following benefits:

**Additional Damage.** Any Custom Job made by you deals an additional die of Weapon Damage. 

**Custom Criticals.** You can roll one additional Damage die when determining the extra damage for a Critical Hit with your Custom Job.

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
#### 15th Level: Lethal Weapon
The sky is the limit when it comes to the damage you can inflict. 
:
You may also roll an additional die of the same size as part of your Weapon Tuning Feature when a dice result during a Damage Roll for an Attack with your Custom Job is the minimum possible value on that dice.

#### 18th Level: Heartseeker
One shot is all it need take you. 
:
Once per Long Rest when you hit with a Ranged Weapon Attack, you can use this Feature (no Action required). If you do, do not make a Damage Roll; instead, each dice you would have rolled instead scores the maximum value on that dice. 

}}

{{imageMaskCenter11,--offsetX:0%,--offsetY:0%,--rotation:0
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/2/24/Poz%C3%ABmka_Elite_2.png?e60777&20230927011040' 
  style='position:absolute;top:210px;right:125px;width:550px' />
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

### Hunter
Hunter Marksmen, much like the profession they share their name with, are opportunists. There is a right moment to strike, and when they do strike, it must be swift, lest another opportunity not be available. Hunter Marksmen embody this, using special Originium technology to enhance their weaponry. The strength it grants is short-lived, and requires time to recharge, but that moment is all they need.

#### 1st Level: Empowered Shots
Hunter Marksmen employ special technology to infuse their weapons with Arts, pushing them beyond what pure mechanics can do alone.
:
**Charges.** You have a maximum of 2 Charges, increasing to 4 at Level 7, and 6 at Level 14. 
:
**Regaining Charges.** You may regain all of your expended Charges as an Action during your Turn, or whenever you finish a Short or Long Rest.
:
**Expending Charges.** Whenever you make a Ranged Weapon Attack, 1 Charge is expended to perform an Empowered Shot. At 1st Level, you may only use the following Empowered Shot; you gain additional Options at 4th Level.
:
- **Piercing Shot.** If the Attack Hits, it deals 1d8 additional Piercing Damage.

{{align-text:left
When you would perform an Empowered Shot against your Target, you do not expend Charge for the first Empowered Shot option you use. This reduction can only occur once per Attack.
}}
#### 1st Level: Energy Retention
You can use the Arts expended in empowering your shots to empower yourself only briefly.
:
Your Speed is increased by 10 ft for each Charge you have expended during the current Turn.

\column

#### 4th Level: Enhanced Empowered Shots
The ways in which you can infuse your Attacks with your technology becomes more versatile. You gain the following benefits:

**Additional Empowered Shots.** You gain the following Empowered Shot options:
- **Concussive Shot:** If the Attack Hits, that Creature must succeed on a Strength Saving Throw against your Martial DC or be knocked 10 feet directly away from you.
- **Dazing Shot:** If the Attack Hits, that Creature must succeed on a Constitution Saving Throw against your Martial DC or be Dazed until the end of their next Turn.
- **Fragmentation Shot:** Other Creatures behind the target Creature from your perspective and within 10 feet of them take 1d8 Thunder Damage.
- **Seeking Shot:** Roll 1d6 and add the result to the result of the Attack Roll for that Attack.

{{align-text:left
**Double Up.** When you perform an Empowered Shot, you may expend a Charge to apply an additional Empowered Shot effect; you may not pick the same option twice for the same Attack.
}}

#### 10th Level: Railgun
You can concentrate your charge into a single, raw blast of Arts.
:
As an Action, you expend any number of Charges, up to the amount you presently have. Then, choose a point you can see within 100 feet; a blast of Originium Arts screams forth from you in a 5 foot wide line to the target point. All Creatures within this line must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, a Creature takes 2d6 Force Damage for each Charge you expended on this Feature. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage.
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have completed a Long Rest.


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

#### 15th Level: Ultimate Empowered Shots
Your Empowered Shots are improved in the following ways:
- **Concussive Shot:** Creatures that fail this Saving Throw also have their Movement Speed halved until the end of their next Turn.
- **Dazing Shot:** Creatures that fail this Saving Throw are now also Blinded until the end of their next Turn.
- **Fragmentation Shot:** Creatures now only need to be within 10 feet of the original Creature, and the Damage is increased to 1d12 Thunder Damage. 
- **Piercing Shot:** Instead deals 2d8 additional Force Damage.
- **Seeking Shot:** Instead adds 2d4 to the result of the Attack Roll.

#### 18th Level: Let the Light In
For brief moments, you and your weapon are as one; man and machine working in concord to the same ends. Your flow state guides you and your weapon to a single goal; punching as many holes in your foes as you can.
:
You may use this Feature as an Action; as part of that Action, you regain all expended Charges. For the next minute, you regain use of your Target Lock Feature at the beginning of each of your Turns, and you may regain all of your expended Charges as a Bonus Action.

:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have completed a Long Rest.

}}

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/b/b0/Coldshot.png?8c9e4d&20230927014734' 
  style='position:absolute;top:380px;right:400px;width:330px' />
  
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/3/34/Ray.png?d0422a&20240109092107' 
  style='position:absolute;top:380px;right:50px;width:330px' />

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

### Spreadshot
Spreadshot Marksmen excel in two aspects; close quarters combat, and combat against tight groups of enemies. Combine these two elements, and you will see results you did not think possible; each blast sweeping away a lesser foe before them.

#### 1st Level: Spread the Love
You gain the following benefits:

**Additional Spread.** If you make an Attack using a Weapon that natively has the Spread Property, you may deal the Piercing Damage to an additional Creature within 5 feet of the original target.

**Up Close and Personal.** You do not suffer Disadvantage for making a Ranged Weapon Attack while there is a hostile Creature within 5 feet of you, as long as the Attack is against one of those Creatures.

**Wide Fire.** Light or Heavy Ranged Weapons gain the Spread Property while they are wielded by you. 

#### 1st Level: Whites Of Their Eyes
Your preferred weaponry is most effective up close and personal; where you can see the whites of their eyes. You gain the following benefits:

**First to the Fray.** Your Walking Speed is increased by 10 feet. During your first Turn after rolling Initiative, your Walking Speed is increased by an additional 10 feet.

**Point Blank.** Your Attacks using weapons with the Spread Property against a Creature within 15 feet of you are considered to be Point Blank Attacks. Point Blank Attacks are made at Advantage, and if the weapon used natively has the Spread Property, deals 1 additional die of Damage. Additionally, if the Creature attacked is also your Target, that Attack has +1 Critical Range.

#### 4th Level: Break Their Lines
You have mastered shooting techniques to better aid you in disrupting the ranks of the enemy, detailed below.
:
**Break Contact.** When a Creature makes a Melee Attack against you, you can make a Ranged Attack against that Creature using a weapon with the Spread Property as a Reaction; if you do so, the Damage of that Attack is halved. Additionally, if you are no longer in the Creature's Reach after this Attack, the triggering Attack misses you automatically.
:
**Gut Shot.** When you make a Point Blank Attack, you can make that Attack a Gut Shot (no Action required). If the Attack hits, it deals additional Damage equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and knocks back that Creature 10 feet. You can use this Feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you complete a Short Rest.

\column

#### 10th Level: Versatility of Violence
You learn how to develop specialised ammunition, known as Shells.
:
**Making Shells.** When you complete a Short Rest, you create a number of Shells equal to your Proficiency Bonus. Shells will degrade to uselessness without maintenance; the maximum amount of Shells you can keep maintained is 10, including loaded Shells. When you create Shells, you choose them in any combination from the list below. Shells have a weight and value of 0.
:
**Using Shells.** A Creature carrying any Shells can load up to five into a weapon with the Spread property they are wielding as a Bonus Action. The maximum number of Shells that can be loaded in a weapon is five. Each time a Creature makes an Attack using that Weapon, a loaded Shell is expended in the order in which they were loaded to modify that Attack in the way detailed below. 

- **Dragon's Breath.** Creatures who take Damage from this Attack must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC or begin Burning.
- **Flechette.** Creatures who take Damage from this Attack must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC or have their Speed halved until the end of their next Turn.
- **Slug.** While this Shell is the next one to be expended, the weapon into which the Shell is loaded has the Loading Property. Attacks with this Shell ignore Damage Reduction; the Loading Property is not removed until the end of that Turn.
- **Beanbag.** Creatures who take Damage from this Attack must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC or become Dazed until the end of their next Turn. If a Creature is reduced to 0 Hit Points by this Attack, they are knocked Unconscious instead of Dying.

{{note
##### Spread and Shells
Damage from the Spread Property is considered to be Damage dealt by that Attack for the purpose of Shells.
}}

#### 15th Level: Breach and Clear
Your attacks are resonant, booming, and lethal. Enemies that survive them find themselves unable to coordinate their counterattack. 
:
A Creature that you have made a Point Blank Attack against cannot take any Reaction against you until the end of the current Turn. A Creature you have Damaged with a Point Blank Attack has Disadvantage on Attacks against you until the end of their next Turn.

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

#### 18th Level: Both Barrels
Give 'em both barrels. 
:
As an Action while you are wielding a weapon that has a Shell loaded, you can unleash a devastating attack. The form of that assault depends on the Shell loaded, as detailed below:
- **Dragon's Breath.** Creatures within a 50 foot Cone originating from you must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take 8d8 Fire Damage and begin Burning. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage and suffer no other effects.
- **Flechette.** Creatures within a 50 foot Cone originating from you must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take 8d8 Piercing Damage, and their Movement Speed is halved. They may reattempt this Saving Throw at the end of each of their Turns, ending this effect on a success. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage, and suffer no other effects. 
- **Slug.** A Creature of your Choice that you can see within 60 feet must make a Constitution Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take Piercing Damage equal to five times the Damage an Attack using that weapon would have dealt to them. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage. Damage dealt by this Feature ignores Damage Reduction.
- **Beanbag.** Creatures within a 50 foot Cone originating from you must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take 8d8 Bludgeoning Damage, and are Dazed. They may reattempt this Saving Throw at the end of each of their Turns, ending this effect on a success. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage, and suffer no other effects. If a Creature is reduced to 0 Hit Points by this Damage, they are knocked Unconscious instead of Dying.

{{align-text:left
Once you have used this Feature, you cannot do so again until you complete a Long Rest.
}}

}}





{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:-5%,--rotation:-9
<img src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/thumb/8/86/Executor_Skin_1.png/1024px-Executor_Skin_1.png?c3c651' 
  style='position:absolute;top:250px;right:-150px;width:1300px;clip-path:rect(auto auto auto auto);' />

{{wide

## Medic
In Londinium's war-torn streets, gentle light radiates from the hands of a hooded Sarkaz woman, closing the wounds of the soldier lying in front of her. She does not know this man. He may even be her enemy; it does not matter. Her sword remains sheathed, and she prays she will never again have to draw it. 
:
As the pioneers return from the site of the accident, terror writ large across their faces, a Liberi moves among these potential Infected with mechanical efficiency. The crystals growing in her skull throb in sympathetic pain, but she pushes the pain down. Some day she will die, but today she can save lives.

}}

### The Selfless, The Unsung
Medical Arts are some of the most difficult Arts to master; a sloppy application can do more harm than good. It requires meticulous study of both Arts and medicine, alongside a healthy helping of natural talent. Many medical Casters are also medical professionals in the traditional sense, though the two are not necessarily synonymous.
:
All this to say, becoming a Medic requires a tremendous amount of work and effort, all for the privilege to put yourself directly in harm's way. Organised conflict might recognise the sacred nature of medics, but most conflict on Terra is not organised, resulting in the exact opposite effect; you are a target. This feat of work all for increased risk all but ensures those who pursue it possess a selfless spirit, willing to risk their own hide to protect others. A Medic is not the one who crushes the foe and leads the charge; they are not the ones whose names will be signed in history. They are the ones that ensure those who do those things make it home to record that history.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/Record_Keeper.png?d6617c' 
  style='position:absolute;top:560px;right:280px;width:500px;clip-path:rect(auto 76% 53% auto);' />

\column

### Class Features

As a Medic, you gain the following Class Features:

#### Hit Points
**Hit Dice:** 1d6 per Medic Level<br>
**Hit Points at 1st Level:** 6 + your Constitution Modifier<br>
**Hit Points at Higher Levels:** 1d6 (or 4) + your Constitution Modifier per Medic Level after 1st

#### Proficiencies
**Armor:** Light Shields<br>
**Weapons:** Light Melee<br>
**Tools:** A Tool Kit/Vehicle Proficiency of your Choice<br>
**Saving Throws:** Choose Two from the following: Constitution, Intelligence, Wisdom  <br>
**Skills:** Medicine + Two from: Lore, Arts, Tech, Insight, Perception, Persuasion

#### Equipment
You start with the following Equipment, in addition to any Equipment granted by your Background. 

- **A)** 1x Light Melee Weapon OR **B)** 1x Light Shield
- **A)** Arts Wand OR **B)** Arts Staff
- One Tool Kit you are Proficient with

OR 

- 1300 LMD

#### 1st Level: Arts Casting
You can cast specialised Arts to preserve life. 
:
***Arts Pool:*** You learn all of the Arts in the Healing Arts Special Arts Pool, and they are always Prepared.
:
***Arts Points:*** The Medic Features table shows how many Arts Points (AP) you have to cast Arts of Level 1 or higher. You regain all expended Arts Points when you finish a Long Rest.
:
***Maximum Arts Level:*** The Medic Features table shows the maximum Level you can cast an Art at, based on your Medic Level. If an Art's Level is above your current Maximum Art Level, you cannot cast it. 
:
***Spellcasting Ability:*** When you gain this Feature, choose either Intelligence or Wisdom; that is your Spellcasting Ability.

{{pageNumber,auto}}

}}

{{align-text:left
#### 1st Level: Life Support
You have a pool of Life Support that replenishes when you finish a Long Rest. The maximum amount of Life Support in your pool at each Level is shown in the Medic Table.
:
As an Action, you can touch a Creature and expend any amount of your current Life Support; the Creature recovers that many missing Hit Points.
:
You can also expend Life Support in the following ways, or in ways determined by your Medic Branch:
:
**Preserver:** When you roll dice to determine the Hit Points recovered by another Creature, or the Temporary Hit Points gained by another Creature, you can expend 5 Life Support to reroll any number of those dice; you must use the new results.
:
**Urgency of Treatment:** When you would cast an Art from your Healing Arts Pool, you can expend Life Support in multiples of 10 to reduce the Arts Point cost of that casting by 1 for every 10 Life Support expended, to a minimum of 0.

#### 1st Level: Medical Expertise
No matter the origin of your medical knowledge, you are adept at saving lives; you gain Expertise in the Medical Skill.

}}

\column

#### 1st Level: Medic Branch
Choose a Medic Branch which most closely fits the style you envision for yourself. Choose one from the following list; further details on these Branches can be found at the end of the Class Description. The Branch you choose grants you Features at Level 1, 4, 7, 11, and 15.
- **Physician:** Physician Medics specialise in healing singular targets, preserving the life of their target at any cost.
- **Therapist:** A Therapist Medic knows that a general treatment is not enough; treatment must be targeted and personalised. They use their powers to modify the effects of their Arts to better suit the situation, and the patient. 
- **Triage:** The battlefield is a chaotic place, and often a great number of people need treating at the same time. Triage Medics specialise in dealing out healing to multiple patients simultaneously, aiming only to preserve as many lives as possible. 
- **Ward:** Prevention is better than cure, or so the adage goes. Ward Medics take this to heart, dishing out barriers that prevent their allies from coming to harm, and ward off Status Conditions.
- **Witchcraft:** While all Medics are potent Casters, Witchcraft Medics weave destruction into their aid, blending offensive Arts and healing.



\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### 2nd Level: Basic Operator Skill
You learn a Basic Operator Skill from the Basic Medic Skills table, or from any other Basic Skill table available to you.

#### 3rd Level: Basic Talent
You gain a Basic Talent of your choice from the Basic Medic Talents table. When you reach 8th Level, you may either pick a different Basic Talent to gain, or upgrade your Basic Talent, gaining its Module Effect.



<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/a/ad/Ansel.png?519bb5&20230926233406' 
  style='position:absolute;top:250px;right:390px;width:440px;clip-path:rect(auto auto 61.5% auto);' />




\column

#### 4th Level: Ability Score Improvement
When you reach 4th Level, and again at 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th, 16th, and 19th Level, you can increase one Ability Score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two Ability Scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can't increase an Ability Score above 20 using this feature.

#### 5th Level: Self-Defence Protocol
It is an unfortunate truth that it is not safe to be a medic everywhere. It is a fortunate truth that you are also capable of casting conventional Arts.
:
When you gain this Feature, choose one of the Standard Arts Pools available to the Caster Class that you have not chosen previously; you learn all of the Arts from that Pool of 5th Level or less, including Cantrips. These Arts cost 1 fewer Arts Point to cast, to a minimum of 0. From 11th Level, these Arts instead cost 2 fewer Arts Points.

#### 7th Level: Advanced Operator Skill
You learn an Advanced Operator Skill from the Advanced Medic Skills table, or from any other Advanced Skill table available to you.

#### 9th Level: Advanced Talent
You gain an Advanced Talent of your choice from the Advanced Medic Talents table. When you reach 13th Level, you may either pick a different Advanced Talent to gain, or upgrade your Advanced Talent, gaining its Module Effect.

#### 12th Level: Resuscitation
In your hands, the borders of life and death begin to blur, and you can pull back those who have just slipped over the edge.
:
Once per Long Rest, you can cast the Revivify Art without expending any Arts Points.

#### 14th Level: Elite Operator Skill
You learn an Elite Operator Skill from the Elite Medic Skills table, or from any other Elite Skill table available to you.

#### 18th Level: To Rob the Ferryman
When you revive someone using the Revivify Art, they recover Hit Points equal to three times your Medic Level.

#### 20th Level: Chosen of Asclepius
Whenever you would roll to determine the amount of Hit Points you restore to a Creature, you can choose not to roll, and instead use the highest number possible for each die. For example, instead of restoring 2d6 Hit Points to a Creature, you restore 12.

{{pageNumber,auto}}

##### Advanced Medic Skills

### Physician
Physicians are the most common of the diaspora of Medics, and for good reason; they reliably deliver potent, targeted aid, pulling their assigned charges back from the brink of death, while able to fight off those who would take advantage of their supposed vulnerability.

#### 1st Level: Focused Treatment
Whenever you roll to determine the amount of Hit Points you restore, or the Damage you deal to only a single Creature, you may add your Proficiency Bonus to the amount restored or dealt. 

#### 4th Level: Intravenous Line
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**Intravenous Line.** As an Action, you can expend 10 Life Support to choose a Creature you can see within 15 feet, and tune your Arts to that Creature's biorhythms for 10 minutes, or until you use this Feature again. An unwilling Creature must make a Constitution Saving Throw against your Spell Save DC, being affected by this feature on a failed Save, or taking 2d8 Necrotic Damage on a successful Save. 
:
While tuned to a Creature, you know the direction that Creature is in, and you can sense when it has less than half of its maximum Hit Points. In addition, once per Turn, when you deal Damage or restore Hit Points to that Creature, roll 1d6; the amount restored or dealt is increased by the amount rolled. 
:
This amount is increased to 2d6 at Level 7. 

#### 7th Level: Hypocritic Oath
All medicine has at least two outcomes, depending on the dose.
:
When you roll to determine the Hit Points restored to a Creature, you may instead choose to harm them. That Creature must make a Constitution Saving Throw against your Spell Save DC; on a failed Save, they instead take Necrotic Damage equal to the amount of Hit Points they would have recovered. On a successful Save, they take half as much Necrotic Damage.
:
This Damage is still considered to be restoring Hit Points for the purpose of any Features that require it. 

#### 11th Level: Inpatient Care
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**Healing Reverberations.** As a Reaction when you roll to determine Hit Points restored to a Creature, you can expend 15 Life Support. At the beginning of your next Turn, that Creature recovers Hit Points equal to half of the amount they recovered from the triggering instance, or 15 Hit Points; whichever is greater. 

#### 15th Level: Surgeon-General
When you restore Hit Points to a single Creature using your Life Support Feature, an Art, or an Operator Skill, the amount of Hit Points you restore is doubled.

  style='position:absolute;top:200px;right:-300px;width:800px' />

### Therapist
Therapy is the art of delivering a treatment to alleviate a specific illness, ailment, or issue. This specificity is where Therapist Medics shine; their Arts may not be as potent or flashy, but they deliver exactly what the patient requires, when they require it.

#### 1st Level: Basic Therapy
The versatility of your Healing Arts are their true merit; you can take these Arts to heights that others cannot. You gain access to the Basic Therapies listed below. You can use these Therapies to temporarily modify Arts that you cast; to use a Therapy, you must expend the amount of Life Support that Therapy costs.
:
You can only use one Therapy option on an Art when you cast it unless noted otherwise. 

#### 4th Level: The Doctor Is In
The distinction of therapy is that it is not a one and done process; it takes time and repetition. This is something you know, and you have refined your skills to facilitate continuous treatment.
:
You can activate this Feature as a Bonus Action. When you do, choose one of your Basic Therapies. For the next minute, you may activate that Therapy option without expending Life Support, and you may use a second Therapy on each Art you cast; you may not use the same option twice.
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have finished a Long Rest.

#### 7th Level: Early Onset Treatment
When an allied Creature you can see takes Damage, you may cast an Art with a Casting Time of an Action or a Bonus Action as a Reaction. That Art must target the damaged Creature.
:
You may use this Feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest.

#### 11th Level: Advanced Therapy
Your treatment has advanced; you gain access to the Advanced Therapies listed below. 

#### 15th Level: Concentrated Treatment
Therapy is about the long term. Sometimes, however, the short term is what matters.
:
When you use your The Doctor Is In Feature, your next 3 Therapies (excluding the one chosen for The Doctor Is In) do not require you to expend Life Support. In addition, when you use one of these 3 free Therapies on an Art that restores Hit Points, you restore an additional 1d6 Hit Points for each Arts Point spent on the casting of that Art.



\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### Basic Therapies
The following Therapies are presented in alphabetical order.

##### Empowering Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art, Creatures affected by that Art deal additional Damage on their next successful Attack within 1 minute equal to your Spellcasting Ability Modifier.

##### Energising Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that targets only one Creature, that Creature can immediately move half their Speed after the Art.

##### Long-Distance Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that has a range of at least 5 feet, you can double the range of the Art. Alternatively, when you cast an Art that has a range of Touch, you can make that Art's range 30 feet.

##### Long-Term Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that has a duration of 1 minute or longer, you can double its duration to a maximum duration of 24 hours.
:
If the affected Art requires Concentration, you have Advantage on any Saving Throw you make to maintain that Concentration.

##### Psychological Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that restores Hit Points, affected Creatures automatically pass the next Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma Saving Throw they make before the beginning of your next Turn.

##### Recuperative Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 5 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that targets only one Creature, for the next minute, that Creature regains Hit Points after taking any Damage from a hostile Creature equal to your Spellcasting Ability Modifier.

\column

#### Advanced Therapies
The following Therapies are presented in alphabetical order.

##### Cleansing Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 10 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that restores Hit Points to a single Creature, you may end one Status Condition affecting that Creature. 

##### Regenerative Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 10 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art that restores Hit Points to a single Creature, for the next minute, that Creature regains 1d6 Hit Points at the beginning of each of their Turns. 

##### Resistance Therapy
**Life Support Cost:** 10 <br>
**Effect:** When you cast an Art, Creatures affected by the Art automatically pass their Saving Throws against Arts and other Art-like effects until the beginning of your next Turn.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/d/db/Nowell.png?20250214085811' 
  style='position:absolute;top:440px;right:-50px;width:575px' />

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### Triage
All Medics specialise in saving lives, of course, but for Triage Medics, it is their primary and foremost concern. Their role is not to nurse a patient back to full health, but to save their life and move on to the next wounded.

#### 1st Level: Rapid Response
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**On the Scene.** As an Action, you can expend 10 Life Support to move up to your Speed without provoking Opportunity Attacks. After you finish this movement, choose a number of other Creatures equal to your Proficiency Bonus that you moved within 5 feet of during this movement (you can only choose each Creature once per use of this Feature), and apply one of the following effects:
- **Heal.** Restore a number of Hit Points to that Creature equal to 1d6 + your Spellcasting Ability Modifier.
- **Harm.** Make a Melee Spell Attack against that Creature as if they were within your Reach; on a hit, that Creature takes Necrotic Damage equal to 1d6 + your Spellcasting Ability Modifier.

{{align-text:left
The amount of Hit Points recovered and Necrotic Damage dealt is increased to 2d6 + your Spellcasting Ability Modifier at 7th Level. 
}}

#### 4th Level: Urgent Care Centre
When the wounded are all around you, you are exactly where you need to be. When you roll to restore Hit Points to yourself with a levelled Art, you can can cause your medical arts to wash out from you in a wave; all other Creatures within 10 feet of you recover Hit Points equal to half of the amount rolled.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/e/ef/Breeze_Elite_2.png?c425c9&20230927015325' 
  style='float:right; margin-right: -70px;width:450px' />  

#### 7th Level: Emergency Medicine
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**Stop the Flow.** As an Action, you can expend 20 Life Support and touch a Creature that is **Dying.** That Creature immediately gains 6 Mettle; this Mettle gain allows them to temporarily exceed the maximum Mettle of 6, to a maximum of 8. Once a Creature has been the target of this Feature, this Feature cannot be used on them again until they are no longer **Dying**.

#### 11th Level: Touch of Life and Death
Your phantasmagoric hands can snatch life and preserve it with the same gentle caress. When you deal Damage or restore Hit Points to a Creature as a result of your On the Scene Feature, you can expend 10 Life Support (no Action required) to cause an additional effect.
- **Heal.** You can end a Condition with a duration of 1 hour or less on that Creature; if the Duration is greater than 1 hour, it is instead paused for 1 hour.
- **Harm.** That Creature is Poisoned until the beginning of your next Turn.

#### 15th Level: Mobile Hospital
Your mere presence is enough to ward off death. 
:
Once per Long Rest, you may activate this Feature as a Bonus Action. For the next minute, your project an aura in a 30-foot radius; the aura moves with you, and is centred on you. When a Creature enters the aura, or the aura enters a Creature's space, you can restore Hit Points to that Creature equal to 3d6 + your Spellcasting Ability Modifier (no Action required). Once you have restored Hit Points to a Creature in this way, they cannot benefit from this Feature again until the beginning of your next Turn.


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

### Ward
Prevention is better than cure is an oft-spoken adage that typically refers to the prevention of disease rather than the treating of it. Ward Medics take this conclusion and extrapolate it, using specially formulated Arts; surely it is better to prevent all injury than to heal it.

#### 1st Level: Prevention, not Cure
Your unique twist on medical Arts is designed to keep people from reaching harm, rather than putting them back together. You gain the following benefits:

**Ward.** Whenever you would roll to Restore Hit Points to a Creature, you can instead grant that same amount of Temporary Hit Points. These Temporary Hit Points are known as your Ward, and interact with your other Features.

**Additional Temporary Hit Points.** Whenever you grant Temporary Hit Points as a result of an Action or Bonus Action, roll 1d8 and add the result to the amount of Temporary Hit Points granted. Additionally, if those Temporary Hit Points were granted as a result of an Art, increased the amount granted by the number of Arts Points spent on that Art.

#### 4th Level: Status Resistance
The first time each Turn a Creature that has your Ward makes a Saving Throw to avoid or end a Status Condition, they do so with Advantage.

#### 7th Level: Treat the Cause, not the Symptom
When a Creature that has your Ward is targeted by an Attack from a Creature within 30 feet of them, you can expend your Reaction to cause the attacking Creature to make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Spell Save DC. On a failed Save, they take 2d10 Force Damage, and the Attack misses that Creature regardless of the result of the roll; on a successful Save, they take half as much Damage, and suffer no other effects.
:
After you use this Feature, you may expend 15 Life Support to restore use of your Reaction.

#### 11th Level: Warding Surge
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**Warding Surge.** As a Reaction when a Creature with your Ward would take Damage, you can expend 10 Life Support to grant that Creature Resistance to those Damage Types until the end of the current Turn.  
:
After you use this Feature, you may expend 15 Life Support to restore use of your Reaction.

\column

#### 15th Level: Community Care
You are the first line of defence for your patients against the world's woes. You gain the following benefits:

**Collective Ward.** When a Creature within 30 feet of you with your Ward would take Damage, you can remove any amount of your Ward from other Creatures within 30 feet of you to reduce that Damage by the amount of Ward removed, to a minimum of 0 (no Action required).

**Ward Regeneration.** If you begin your Turn and do not have your Ward, you can gain Temporary Hit Points equal to half of your Medic Level, rounded up; this is considered to be your Ward.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/7/75/Harold.png?78f826&20231205060843' 
  style='position:absolute;top:260px;right:0px;width:400px' />

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

\column
:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
### Witchcraft
"Do no Harm" does not come into the vernacular of a Witchcraft Medic. They strive to do as much harm as possible to their enemies, harnessing and recycling the energy of their offensive Arts to fuel their healing.

#### 1st Level: Halfway House
You bridge the gap between offensive and medical Arts, gaining the following benefits:

**Additional Arts Pool.** Choose one of the Standard Arts Pools available to the Caster Class. You learn all of the Arts in that Pool, and they are always prepared for you. From 5th Level, these Arts cost 1 fewer Arts Point to cast, to a minimum of 0. From 11th Level, these Arts instead cost 2 fewer Arts Points.

**Healing through Harm.** As a Reaction, when you roll to determine the Damage of an Art, you may add half of that value to your Life Support Pool; this allows you to temporarily exceed your maximum Life Support; any additional Life Support is lost when you complete a Long Rest. Once you have used this Feature, you must complete a Short Rest before you may do so again.

#### 4th Level: Witchdoctor
You further refine your technique for "supporting" your allies through your Arts. You gain the following benefits:

**Operator Skill Access.** Whenever you gain a new Operator Skill, you may instead choose a Skill from the appropriate Operator Skills table available to the Caster Class.

**Improved Life Support.** The range at which you can restore Hit Points with your Life Support Feature is increased to 60 feet.

{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:1%,--rotation:90
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/2/22/Reed_the_Flame_Shadow_Elite_2.png?45811a&20230926224241' 
  style='position:absolute;top:-100px;right:-350px;width:1700px' />
}}

{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:10%,--rotation:180

  style='position:absolute;top:-100px;right:-350px;width:1700px' />
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

#### 7th Level: Witchcurse
You gain an additional way to use your Life Support Feature:

**Witchcurse.** As a Bonus Action, you can expend 10 Life Support to choose a Creature you can see within 60 feet and place a Witchcurse upon them for 1 minute, or until you place another Witchcurse. The Witchcurse causes that Creature to experience greater harm than they perhaps are; the first time each Turn a Creature with your Witchcurse takes Arts Damage, roll 2d6; you may restore that many Hit Points to a Creature you can see within 60 feet, and the Creature with your Witchcurse takes Psychic Damage equal to the amount of Hit Points you restored.

#### 11th Level: Life Support Specialist
You gain the following benefits:

**Efficient Life Support.** When you cast a levelled Art, you gain Life Support equal to twice the Arts Points you expended to cast that Art, and you may restore Hit Points with your Life Support Feature as an Action or Bonus Action this Turn.
   
**Witch's Boon.** If you restore at least 5 Hit Points to a Creature with your Life Support Feature, until the end of that Creature's next Turn, they deal additional 1d10 Force Damage with their Attacks.

#### 15th Level: Life through Death
When you roll to determine the Damage of an Art or Operator Skill, you can choose not to roll, and instead deal the maximum amount possible. If you do, you can then restore a number of Hit Points equal to the amount rolled. Choose a number of Creatures you can see within 60 feet of you, and divide those Hit Points between them.
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you finish a Long Rest.

}}

{{imageMaskEdge5,--offset:-7%,--rotation:0

  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/5/5b/Hibiscus_the_Purifier_Elite_2.png?ba650b&20230927001357' 
  style='position:absolute;top:300px;right:-100px;width:1000px;clip-path:rect(11.5% auto auto auto);' />

## Specialist

Fathoms below the sea, an Aegir dances amongst her Seaborn foe, her spinning saw blade claiming her pound of flesh with each move. Her foes stumble in confusion; are they seeing one spectre, or two?
:
Rain-slick Siracusa is as it ever was. In its pitch-dark shadow, a Lupo and Vulpo clash blades; an unfortunate crossing of paths, but the Lupo never lets anything get in the way of her target.
:
In the heights of Lungmen, a Cautus clambers through a window with the aid of her grappling hook, ready to ransack this den of evil of all its worth.

### Powerfully Individual 
It is nearly impossible to summate Specialists in a succinct manner. Some describe them as those that simply do not fit under a more defined heading. While there is some truth to this, it does not mean Specialists have not felt a calling. Specialists are those so dedicated to a particular style, often an unconventional one, that to place them under a more established heading would do both a disservice. The application of a Specialist may be narrow, but when they are within their zone, you will never wish for anyone else.

{{tocExcludeH3 
### Class Features
}}

As a Specialist, you gain the following Class Features:

#### Hit Points
**Hit Dice:** 1d8 per Specialist Level<br>
**Hit Points at 1st Level:** 8 + your Constitution Modifier<br>
**Hit Points at Higher Levels:** 1d8 (or 5) + your Constitution Modifier per Specialist Level after 1st

#### Proficiencies
**Armor:** Light Armour, Medium Armour, Light Shields<br>
**Weapons:** Light Melee, Light Ranged<br>
**Tools:** Two Tool Kits of your Choice<br>
**Saving Throws:** Dexterity + one of your choice from the following: Wisdom, Charisma <br>
**Skills:** Three from: Acrobatics, Sleight of Hand, Stealth, Tech, Perception, Wilderness

#### Equipment
You start with the following Equipment, in addition to any Equipment granted by your Background. 

- **A)** 1x Light Melee Weapon OR **B)** 1x Light Ranged Weapon
- **A)** 1x Light Melee Weapon OR **B)** 1x Light Shield
- **A)** Reinforced Outfit OR **B)** Underarmour
- 1x Tool Kit you are Proficient with

OR

- 1300 LMD

{{classTable,frame,decoration
##### The Specialist
| Level | Proficiency Bonus | Features |    
|:-----:|:-----------------:|:--------:|
|  1st  |  +2  | Specialist Branch, Weapon Specialisation
|  2nd  |  +2  | Basic Operator Skill
|  3rd  |  +2  | Basic Talent
|  4th  |  +2  | Ability Score Improvement, Show Your Style
|  5th  |  +3  | Branch Feature
|  6th  |  +3  | Ability Score Improvement, Weapon Adept
|  7th  |  +3  | Elite 1- Advanced Operator Skill
|  8th  |  +3  | Ability Score Improvement, Basic Talent Module
|  9th  |  +4  | Advanced Talent
| 10th  |  +4  | Ability Score Improvement
| 11th  |  +4  | Branch Feature
| 12th  |  +4  | Ability Score Improvement, Weapon Expertise
| 13th  |  +5  | Advanced Talent Module
| 14th  |  +5  | Elite 2- Elite Operator Skill
| 15th  |  +5  | Branch Feature
| 16th  |  +5  | Ability Score Improvement
| 17th  |  +6  | Falling Upwards
| 18th  |  +6  | Weapon Mastery
| 19th  |  +6  | Ability Score Improvement
| 20th  |  +6  | Branch Feature
}}

#### 1st Level: Specialist Branch
Choose a Specialist Branch which most closely fits the style you envision for yourself. Choose one from the following list; detail on these Branches can be found at the end of the Class Description. Your Branch grants you Features at Level 1, 5, 11, 15, and 20.
- **Executor:** Executor Specialists are cloak and dagger experts; some fight to take life, others to preserve it. 
- **Hookmaster:** Hookmasters wield a weapon on a long rope like an extension of their own body, forcing their enemies into disadvantageous positions.
- **Infusion:** Genius comes with a price; Infusion Specialists formulate powerful "medicinal" concoctions that help and harm in equal measure.
- **Inventor:** The Inventor modifies weapons and armours with modifications of questionable safety.
- **Skyranger:** Armed with a jetpack and a cavalier disregard for their own safety, Skyranger Specialists take to the skies, combatting drones on their turf.
- **Dollkeeper:** The unique Arts of a Dollkeeper are often a well-kept secret; Arts they use to manipulate a construct that looks and acts just like them. 
- **Trapmaster:** Trapmaster Specialists excel in the curious art of setting and baiting traps of all kinds.


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

#### 1st Level: Weapon Specialisation
Choose either Melee Combat or Ranged Combat; you gain benefits based on your selection, with additional benefits at Levels 6, 12, and 18. 

}}

**Melee Combat.** Choose one of the following options (You can’t take a Fighting Style option more than once, even if you later get to choose again).

##### Brawler
On each of your Turns, you may make an Unarmed Strike as a Bonus Action.

##### Deadly Fighter
The first time each Turn you make an Attack Roll using a Weapon with the Deadly Property, the Critical Range of that Attack is increased by 1.

##### Defensive
When you are hit by an Attack while you have a Melee Weapon or Shield equipped, you may use your Reaction to increase your AC by 1d4 for the duration of that Attack, possibly causing that Attack to miss.

##### Duelling
You have a +1 bonus to Attack and Damage Rolls for Melee Attacks against the first Creature you Attack each Turn.

##### Great Weapon Fighting
You gain a +2 bonus to Damage rolls you make with Melee Weapons that you are wielding with two hands. The weapon must have the Two-Handed or Versatile Quality for you to gain this benefit.

##### Lightly Armed
When you are wielding a Melee Weapon in one hand and no other Weapons or Shields, you gain a +2 Bonus to Attack Rolls with that Weapon.

##### Spearwall
When you make a Melee Attack while it is not your Turn, that Attack deals an additional dice of Damage.

##### Two-Weapon Fighting
When you engage in Two-Weapon Fighting, you can add your Ability Modifier to the Damage of the additional Attack(s), and you may draw or stow two one-handed weapons when you would normally be able to draw or stow only one.

\column

**Ranged Combat.** Choose one of the following options (You can’t take a Fighting Style option more than once, even if you later get to choose again).
:
##### Braced for Action
Once per Turn, when you make a Damage Roll for a Weapon with the Brace Keyword, you may reroll one of those dice.

##### Close and Personal
You do not have Disadvantage on Ranged Attack Rolls when there is a hostile Creature within 5 feet of you.

##### Cone of Fire
Whenever you make an Attack using the Spread Quality, you may deal the additional Damage even if the Attack misses.

##### Deadly Fighter
The first time each Turn you make an Attack Roll using a Weapon with the Deadly Property, the Critical Range of that Attack is increased by 1.

##### Ranger
You gain a +2 Bonus to Attack Rolls you make with Ranged or Thrown Weapons.

##### Smooth Operator
When you make an Attack as a Bonus Action using the Rapid Fire Property, you may add your Ability Modifier to the Damage Roll of the additional Attack(s). 

##### Two-Weapon Fighting
When you engage in Two-Weapon Fighting, you can add your Ability Modifier to the Damage of the additional Attack(s), and you may draw or stow two one-handed weapons when you would normally be able to draw or stow only one.

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### 2nd Level: Basic Operator Skill
You learn a Basic Operator Skill from the Basic Specialist Skill table, or from any other Basic Skill table available to you.

#### 3rd Level: Basic Talent
You gain a Basic Talent of your choice from the Basic Specialist Talents table. When you reach 8th Level, you may either pick a different Basic Talent to gain, or upgrade your Basic Talent, gaining its Module Effect.

#### 4th Level: Ability Score Improvement
When you reach 4th level, and again at 6th, 8th, 10th, 12th, 16th, and 19th level, you can increase one Ability Score of your choice by 2, or you can increase two Ability Scores of your choice by 1. As normal, you can’t increase an Ability Score above 20 using this feature.

#### 4th Level: Show your Style
As a Specialist, you do one thing, and you do it very well; you love nothing more than exercising that talent. This talent is represented by your Prowess. 
:
**Gaining Prowess.** Your maximum Prowess is equal to your Proficiency Bonus. You gain 1 Prowess whenever you complete a Short or Long Rest. You can additionally gain Prowess by fulfilling conditions based on your Specialist Branch, outlined in that Branch. 
:
**Spending Prowess.** You can expend your Prowess for the option presented here, or for any other Feature that requires it:
:
**Mark of a Specialist.** When you fail on a Skill Check using a Skill you are Proficient in, you may expend 1 Prowess to reroll the result of that Check, possibly turning it into a success. If you still fail the Check, your Prowess is refunded, but you cannot reroll that Check again.

#### 6th Level: Weapon Adept
You gain additional benefits based on your Weapon Specialisation.

**Melee Combat.** When you take the Attack Action, you may make one additional Attack as part of that Action; this Attack must be a Melee Attack. Additionally, when you roll a 1 or 2 on one or more Damage dice for a Melee Attack, you can reroll those dice; you must use the new results. 

**Ranged Combat.** When you take the Attack Action, you may make one additional Attack as part of that Action; this Attack must be a Ranged Attack. Additionally, when you roll a 1 for a Ranged Attack Roll, you may reroll that die; you must use the new result.

\column

#### 7th Level: Advanced Operator Skill
You learn an Advanced Operator Skill from the Advanced Specialist Skill table, or from any other Advanced Skill table available to you.

#### 9th Level: Advanced Talent
You gain an Advanced Talent of your choice from the Advanced Specialist Talents table. When you reach 13th Level, you may either pick a different Advanced Talent to gain, or upgrade your Advanced Talent, gaining its Module Effect.

#### 12th Level: Weapon Expertise
You gain additional benefits based on your Weapon Specialisation.

**Melee Combat.** Your Critical Range for Melee Attacks is improved by 1. When you roll to determine the Damage of a Melee Attack, you may expend 1 Prowess as a Bonus Action to roll one additional Damage die and add it to the total Damage. 

**Ranged Combat.** Whenever you have Advantage on a Ranged Attack Roll, you may roll an additional die, keeping only the die with the highest result. Additionally, you may expend 1 Prowess as a Bonus Action to give yourself Advantage on the next Ranged Attack Roll you make.

#### 14th Level: Elite Operator Skill
You learn an Elite Operator Skill from the Elite Specialist Skill table, or from any other Elite Skill table available to you.

#### 17th Level: Falling Upwards
Success and failure are simply mindsets; a mindset can be changed. You gain the following way to expend your Prowess: 

**It's All Skill.** When you would make a Saving Throw, you can expend 3 Prowess as a Reaction to succeed on that Saving Throw, as if you had rolled the minimum amount required; you must do so before you roll the Saving Throw. 

#### 18th Level: Weapon Mastery
You gain additional benefits based on your Weapon Specialisation.

**Melee Combat.** Once per Turn when you hit with a Melee Attack Roll, you can cause that Attack to deal additional Weapon Damage equal to half your Specialist Level. 

**Ranged Combat.** Once per Turn when you make a Ranged Attack Roll, you can double your Proficiency Bonus for the purpose of that Ranged Attack Roll only.

| Oath	| 80	| 40	| Defensive	| Action	| You may activate this Operator Skill even if you would be prevented from taking an Action. You may remove any Status Conditions or effects affecting you, and you gain Temporary Hit Points equal to your Wisdom Modifier multiplied by ten (minimum of 10). While you have this Temporary Hit Points, you take half Damage from all sources. These Temporary Hit Points are removed when the Skill ends.	| 4 Rounds
| Silencer	| 65	 | 35	| Offensive	| Bonus Action	| When you make an Attack Roll, you may roll that dice two additional times, keeping the highest result and discarding the others. In addition, your Critical Range is improved by 1.  	| 3 Rounds
| Torrential Downpour	| 65	| 30	| Automatic	| Action	| When you activate this Operator Skill, and at the beginning of each of your Turns for the duration, up to 5 different Creatures of your choice within 30 feet of you must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC as your Arts hammer upon them like rain. On a failed Save, they take 3d6 Force Damage; on a successful Save, they take half as much Damage.	| 3 Rounds
| When Space Obeys	| 65	| 30	| Automatic	| Action	| You create a vacuum effect by slashing the very air with your Arts; Creatures of your choice within 30 feet of you must make a Strength Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take 10d6 Slashing Damage and are pulled up to 20 feet directly towards you. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage, and are only pulled 10 feet towards you. After this Damage is dealt, you may immediately expend your Bonus Action to teleport to a location you can see within 60 feet.	| Instant

}}

Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have completed a Long Rest.	| When you gain these Temporary Hit Points, you also take half Damage from the triggering instance of Damage.

### Executor
An Executor Specialist's primary concern is their mission, whatever it may be. Skulking in the shadows, waiting for their moment, before fading away once more. As a result, they are often not the most sociable people but if you need a target dead, or a target preserved, you can ask for no better.

#### 1st Level: The Wolf and the Shepherd
Choose your path, and walk it until the end. You gain the following benefits:

**To Hunt, or Not To Hunt.** When you adopt this Branch, you must choose whether you will be a Hunter or a Bodyguard; your Branch Features grant you different benefits based on your choice. 

**Deathmark.** You have a Deathmark that grants you benefits based on whether you or another Creature has it; you can transfer your Deathmark from yourself to a hostile Creature by making an Attack against them.
:
Your Deathmark returns to you when a Creature with your Deathmark is reduced to 0 Hit Points, or at the end of your Turn if you have not Attacked or dealt Damage to a hostile Creature with your Deathmark. When your Deathmark returns to you, you gain 3 Skill Points, and you can immediately take the Disengage Action as a Reaction. 
:
While you have your Deathmark, you gain 3 Skill Points at the beginning of your Turn in combat. Additionally, you can take the Hide Action as a Bonus Action. 
:
While another Creature has your Deathmark, you deal 1d4 additional Damage to that Creature when you hit them with an Attack; this additional Damage is increased to 2d4 at Level 7, and 3d4 at Level 14. Furthermore, you gain an additional benefit against a Creature with your Deathmark based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:

- **Hunter:** The Critical Range of your Attacks against Creatures with your Deathmark is increased by 1.
- **Bodyguard:** When a hostile Creature with your Deathmark would make an Attack against a Creature while you are both within the Reach or Range of that Attack and also within 10 feet of an original target, you can cause that Attack to target you instead of that Creature (no Action required). 

#### 5th Level: In Every Shadow
You cannot be impeded by such petty things as terrain. You gain the following benefits:

**Additional Movement Speeds.** You gain a Climbing and Swimming Speed equal to your Walking Speed.

**Stealth Expertise.** You gain Proficiency in the Stealth Skill. If you already have Proficiency, you instead gain Expertise. 


\column



#### 5th Level: Spring the Ambush/Escape the Ambush
You gain one of the following ways to expend your Prowess based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:

**Hunter (Sweeper).** While you are Hiding or Invisible, you can expend 1 Prowess as an Action. If you do so, until the end of the Turn your Reach is increased by 5 feet, and you may immediately make a Melee Attack against each Creature of your Choice within your Reach; you can only make one Attack against each Creature. If you hit a Creature with an Attack as part of this Action, that Creature immediately gains your Deathmark, even if it is on another Creature. At the end of the Action, your Deathmark returns to you. 

**Bodyguard (Smokescreen).** You can expend 1 Prowess as a Reaction to create a 10-foot-radius sphere of Arts-infused smoke centred at your feet. This smoke lasts until the end of your next Turn, and is considered to be Magical Darkness. Additionally, when you create this smoke, you can choose one allied Creature within it; they may use their Reaction to move up to their Speed. 
:
**Gaining Prowess as an Executor.** As an Executor, you gain 1 Prowess when a Creature with your Deathmark is reduced to 0 Hit Points. Once you have gained Prowess in this way, you may not do so again until the beginning of your next Turn. 

You also can gain Prowess in an additional way based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:
- **Hunter:** When you deal 30 or more Damage with a single Attack, you gain 1 Prowess. 
- **Bodyguard:** At the end of an allied Creature's Turn where they were Attacked while within 10 feet of you, but have taken no Damage from hostile Creatures that Turn, you gain 1 Prowess.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/2/28/Projekt_Red_Elite_2.png?ed557f&20230926225300' 
  style='position:absolute;top:660px;right:20px;width:400px' />

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### 11th Level: Preysense/Dangersense
You gain one of the following benefits based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:

**Hunter.** When you score a Critical Hit, you may roll 1 additional Damage die and add it to the extra Damage inflicted by that Critical Hit. Additionally, when you score a Critical Hit against a living Creature, you also inflict the Bleeding Condition. 

**Bodyguard.** When an allied Creature within 5 feet of you is subjected to an effect that allows them to make a Strength or Dexterity Saving Throw to take only half Damage, you can use your Reaction to choose a location within a distance equal to half of your Speed; both you and that Creature move to that location as close together as possible. If you moved to a location outside of that Area of Effect, both Creatures instead take no Damage if they succeed on the Saving Throw and only half Damage if they fail.

#### 15th Level: In for the Kill/Blade Shield
You gain one of the following benefits based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:

**Hunter.** When you transfer your Deathmark to a hostile Creature, that Creature is Vulnerable against all Damage dealt by your Attacks until the end of the Turn.

**Bodyguard.** As a Reaction when a Creature with your Deathmark makes an Attack Roll against you while they are within your Reach, you can make a Weapon Attack against that Creature. If your Attack hits, subtract your Proficiency Bonus from the result of that Creature's Attack Roll, possibly causing it to miss.

#### 20th Level: Mortal Blow/Ultimate Sword and Shield
No matter your path, you have reached its end. Only death can keep you from your mission now. 
:
You gain one of the following benefits based on your choice of Hunter or Bodyguard:

**Hunter.** Once per Long Rest as an Action, choose a Creature you can see within a distance equal to your Speed; you flash towards them with a lethal strike. You teleport to an unoccupied space within 5 feet of that Creature, and they must make a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. Roll 10d8. On a failed Save, that Creature loses Hit Points equal to the amount rolled. On a successful Save, they lose Hit Points equal to half the amount rolled. If the Creature has the Bleeding Condition, they lose twice as many Hit Points.

**Bodyguard.** You are Resistant to all Damage dealt by a hostile Creature with your Deathmark. Additionally, using your Blade Shield Feature no longer expends your Reaction. Finally, when another  Creature within 10 feet of you is targeted for an Attack by a hostile Creature, you can transfer your Deathmark, wherever it is, to the Attacking Creature as a Reaction.

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/0/07/Crownslayer.png?20241103015747' 
  style='position:absolute;top:90px;right:50px;width:300px' />
  
<img

### Hookmaster
It is not a requirement for Specialists to be somewhat unhinged, but it does help. The battle style of a Hookmaster, complete with weapon too complex for even experienced warriors to make sense of, and combined with their death-defying feats of derring-do, certainly does not help that impression.

#### 1st Level: Hooked
You can convert a Melee Weapon into a Hook to be used with the rest of your Features; the weapon you choose must not have the Two-Handed Property, and it must deal Slashing or Piercing Damage. When you choose this Branch, you may convert one Melee Weapon from your Starting Equipment that meets these criteria for free. If you need to replace your Hook, or want additional Hooks, it requires 200 LMD, an hour of uninterrupted time, and a Melee Weapon to convert.
:
A Weapon that has been converted into a Hook gains the following effects:
- Only Creatures with this Feature are considered to be Proficient with the Hook. 
- Its Thrown Property, if it has one, is replaced with Thrown (30/-). If it does not have the Thrown Property, it gains the Thrown (30/-) Property. When you make an Attack with this Weapon using the Thrown Property, it immediately returns to you after the Attack.
- Your Hook gains the Two-Handed Property.
- When you make a Ranged Attack using your Hook, you may use your Strength Bonus instead of Dexterity for the Attack and Damage Rolls. 
- When you hit a Creature no more than one size larger than you with a Ranged Attack using your Hook, you can pull that Creature 10 feet towards you.
- You can make a Ranged Attack using your Hook as a Bonus Action. You cannot make, or have made, any Ranged Attacks with your Hook on a Turn you do this.


{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:1%,--rotation:90
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/4/44/Cliffheart_Skin_1.png?88673d&20230927024526' 
  style='position:absolute;top:30px;right:-10px;width:1250px' />
}}

{{imageMaskEdge1,--offset:22%,--rotation:0
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/4/44/Cliffheart_Skin_1.png?88673d&20230927024526' 
  style='position:absolute;top:30px;right:-10px;width:1250px' />
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### 5th Level: Covering Ground, Covering Options. 
Your practice with your Hook grants you the following benefits:

**Big Boot.** When you hit a Creature no more than one size larger than you with an Unarmed Strike, you can push that Creature 10 feet directly away from you.

**King of the Urban Jungle.** During your Turn, if you have moved at least 20 feet immediately beforehand, you can choose a point on a surface you can see within range of your Hook (no Action required). If you do, you gain the following effects; these effects expire after you move, or at the end of your Turn:
- You cannot attack with your Hook.
- You gain a Flying Speed equal to twice the Range of your Hook. However, you cannot move further away from the chosen point than the range of your Hook, you cannot move any higher than the chosen point, and you must move at least 10 feet from the location you started flying.

{{align-text:left
If you deal Damage with a Melee Attack to a Creature immediately after making this flying movement, that Creature takes additional Damage equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and if the Creature would be forcibly moved as a result of that Damage, the distance they are moved is doubled.
}}

<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/b/b4/Rope.png?c9130d&20230927012101' 
  style='position:absolute;top:520px;right:350px;width:500px' />

\column

#### 5th Level: Ensnare and Entangle
Your prowess with your Hook is such that no beast great or small can escape its entangling grasp. You gain the following benefits:
:
**Bound in Rope.** As an Action while you have your Hook equipped, you can expend 1 Prowess to choose a Creature no more than one size larger than you that you can see within range of your Hook; that Creature must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC or become Grappled by you. A Creature Grappled in this way can use their Action to reattempt the Saving Throw, ending the effect on a success. The Condition also ends if you are further away than the range of your Hook, or if you end it during your Turn (no Action required).
:
While you have a Creature Grappled in this way, you can use your Bonus Action to drag them up to 15 feet towards you. While they are within 5 feet of you, you can hogtie that Creature as an Action; this has the same effect as applying Manacles to that Creature. While you have a Creature Grappled in this way, you cannot use that Hook to make Attacks, or to use your Features. 
:
**Wrangler.** As an Action while you have your Hook equipped, you can expend 1 Prowess to choose a Creature that is more than one size larger than you. That Creature must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC, or your Hook latches onto them. When this occurs, you may immediately move to that Creature's space; for the duration, you may occupy the same space as that Creature. When the Creature you are hooked onto moves, you move along with them without consuming your own movement. 
:
While you are hooked onto that Creature, your Attacks against only that Creature are made at Advantage. Additionally, you can take a -5 to the Attack Roll for an Attack against only that Creature to cause that Attack to ignore any Damage Reduction that Creature has.
:
At the end of their Turn, a Creature you are hooked to may reattempt the Saving Throw, shaking you off on a successful Save, ending this effect, and possibly causing you to take Damage from falling. Additionally, you may end the effect during your Turn (no Action required).
:
**Gaining Prowess as a Hookmaster.** As a Hookmaster, you gain 1 Prowess whenever you cause a Creature to take Damage from the environment, such as a result of falling or standing in a burning area. Additionally, you gain 1 Prowess at the end of a Turn you have moved more than 45 feet with a Flying Speed. 

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

#### 11th Level: Mighty Push
As an Action, choose a Creature you can see within 5 feet; that Creature must make a Strength Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, they take 8d6 Bludgeoning Damage, and are knocked back 20 feet. On a successful Save, they take half as much Damage and are pushed back only half as much. If they collide with a wall or other solid surface as part of this movement, they take Damage as if they had fallen that distance. If they collide with another Creature, they both take Damage as if they had fallen that distance.
:
You can use this Feature a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and regain all expended uses when you complete a Long Rest.

#### 15th Level: Spider's Essence
Your familiarity with verticality makes even arachnids look like beginners. You gain the following benefits:

**Longer Hook.** The Thrown Property of your Hooks is increased to Thrown (50/-)

**Spider's Thread.** As an Action, you can use your Hook to grapple to a surface of your choice above you, granting yourself a Climbing Speed until the beginning of your next Turn equal to the length of your Hook, but only when you are climbing towards your chosen point from the location you used this Feature. Additionally, until the beginning of your next Turn, other Creatures that begin climbing from the same location towards the same point also gain a climbing Speed equal to your Hook's Range until the end of their Turn.

**Swing to Safety.** You can now use your King of the Urban Jungle Feature as a Reaction while you are falling, as long as the height you are falling is enough for you to take falling Damage. 

#### 20th Level: Scorpion Sting
As an Action while you have your Hook equipped, choose a Creature you can see within range of your Hook. That Creature must succeed on a Dexterity Saving Throw against your Martial DC. On a failed Save, you pull that Creature directly towards you until they are within 5 feet of you if they are no more than one size larger than you; if they are larger than this, you pull yourself directly towards them until you are within 5 feet of them. 
After this movement, you can make 6 Attacks with your Hook against that Creature with Advantage. Each of these Attacks deals additional Weapon Damage equal to half your Specialist Level. 
:
If you have a Creature that is either Grappled by you or you are hooked onto to, you can use this Feature on that Creature, ignoring restrictions limiting use of your Hook. If you do so, that Creature automatically fails their Saving Throw against this Feature.
:
Once you have used this Feature, you may not do so again until you have completed a Long Rest.

{{imageMaskEdge5,--offset:3%,--rotation:270
<img 
  src='https://arknights.wiki.gg/images/e/e1/Gladiia_Skin_1.png?9c00e2&202309270005169' 
  style='position:absolute;top:0px;right:-450px;width:1300px;clip-path:rect(auto auto auto 33%);' />
